2025–26 changes and corrections

Recorded changes to free tiers, trial credits and startup programs, and corrections from the critics pass. Checked 2026-10-22.

Changes

Recorded 2025–26 changes by provider
ProviderChange
500 Global1) No standalone public free cloud-credit program, free tier, or education license exists at 500 Global — the "Startup Cloud-Credit Programs (VC/Accelerator Funded)" front is realized only as bundled partner perks (the 500 FounderHub platform, 400+ perks, "$1M+ in credits" claim on the official Eurasia page) that are reachable exclusively by 500-affiliated founders; nothing is open to students or the general public. 2) Flagship deal restructured: the historical ~$50K–$100K for 5–7% (and 2025 third-party listings citing "$150K for 6%, $37.5K fee") is no longer published; the current official flagship page (read today, 2026-10-22) states "up to $50K right away, up to $1M more as you progress" with a $35K Phase 1 program fee — so known-so-far numbers are stale. 3) LatAm: the Nov 2025 call was "$300K for 10% to 5 startups" (contxto, Nov 11, 2025); the official page now shows a $300K investment commitment with a July 31, 2026 deadline, equity undisclosed. 4) Domain note: www.500global.com refused connections (ECONNREFUSED on a DigitalOcean IP, Oct 22, 2026) — the official site of record is 500.co; treat 500global.com as parked/unreliable. 5) No 2025–2026 sunset of the FounderHub perks platform itself; credit amounts are partner-controlled and unpublished.
a16za16z is a VC, not a SaaS provider: it has NO standalone free tier, trial, or education (.edu) license of its own. Every "free offer" routes through third-party provider programs where a16z acts as an investor/provider/referrer (AWS Activate, Microsoft for Startups, Alchemy) or through its own Speedrun residency. Active as of 2026-10-22: (1) a16z is a confirmed AWS Activate Provider — Portfolio tier is currently capped at up to $200K; self-funded (Founders) tier only up to $5K; AI-startup credits are invite-only. (2) a16z Speedrun is not sunset — SR008 is scheduled Feb 1 - Apr 16/18, 2027 in San Francisco, applications closing Nov 1, 2026. (3) The a16z x Alchemy onchain program is active but explicitly excludes teams that already received $5K+ in AWS credits (no double-dipping). NOT completed this session: verification of a16z's Microsoft for Startups / Azure (~$150K) referral leg — the Microsoft page 404'd and the shared web-search budget was exhausted, so that finding is low-confidence and should be re-verified. No student-specific free offer from a16z was found.
AdyenFor Adyen (Payments & Fintech Dev Tools), of the four requested categories only the free test/sandbox environment + test-mode API access is a genuine, currently-available free offer. Verifications (all done via WebFetch of official Adyen pages + Adyen's docs sitemap + two third-party directories; the second WebSearch hit was blocked because the session web-search budget was exhausted): (1) No perpetual FREE production tier - adyen.com/pricing is per-transaction (cards ~US$0.13 + Interchange++ + 0.60%), 'no setup fee / no monthly fees,' with a per-industry minimum invoice; published fees are indicative, not guaranteed. (2) No 'trial credits' program - Adyen's dev flow is the self-service test/sandbox, not a time-boxed product trial. (3) Startup program: the 'Adyen Accelerator' (impact/social startups, EMEA, 2021-2022, mentorship/visibility, no fee) has no publicly confirmed 2025-2026 cohort and appears dormant; an 'Adyen for Startups' credits offer exists only as an unconfirmed third-party listing. (4) No student/education license or .edu discount found anywhere on Adyen's site or docs. The Adyen developer-docs sitemap (docs.adyen.com) contains NO page matching 'startup,' 'student,' 'education,' 'trial,' or 'free credit.' No material 2025-2026 change was detected: the 'no monthly fee / per-transaction / free test environment' model persisted.
AirplaneFULLY SUNSET - nothing live as of 2026-10-22. Timeline (verified today via DNS, live probing and Wayback): (1) 2024-01-03: Airtable announces acquisition of Airplane in official blog 'Airtable acquires Airplane' (archived: https://web.archive.org/web/20240310140454/https://www.airplane.dev/blog/airtable); same post states 'we will unfortunately be sunsetting the Airplane platform on March 1' and that new signups had already closed. (2) 2024-03-01: platform sunset date. (3) 2024-05-16: /startups already returning 404. (4) No 200-status HTML captures of any airplane.dev page (incl. subdomains) exist after mid-March 2024; all 2025-2026 captures are 301s. (5) As of 2026-10-22 the domain itself is lapsed/parked: authoritative Googledomains NS shows apex A-record 198.49.23.145 (NSI-style parking IP) and NXDOMAIN for www.airplane.dev; www paths serve self-referential 301 loops behind a 'Server: Squarespace' header, apex 301s to www - a broken-redirect parking loop, so even the parked page won't render. GitHub orgs 'rethink-dev' and 'getairplane' both 404. The team was absorbed into Airtable; Airtable did not continue Airplane as a standalone product on that domain. No education/student offer existed at any point: no /education, /students, or /student-programs pages in the archive and zero 'student'/'.edu' mentions on archived pricing/startup pages. Note the sunsetting happened in 2024, not 2025-2026; during 2025-2026 the domain simply went from dead to parked - no offer changes. All claims above therefore rely on archived copies of the official pages (read today), not on live ones, since the live pages no longer exist.
AirshipNo verifiable free offer (free tier, trial credits, startup program, or education license) exists on Airship's official pages as of 2026-10-22. (1) The known 'Free tier: 100 MAU (Engage)' appears SUNKEN: the product name 'Engage' no longer appears anywhere on airship.com (checked nav, /pricing/, /docs/, homepage, /company/faqs/) — the product line was rebranded into 'Airship Experience Platform' (AXP) and 'Airship Essentials'. The current pricing page (https://www.airship.com/pricing/) lists only two tiers: AXP (enterprise, quote-form only) and Essentials (self-service registration at go.airship.com/accounts/onboarding/register/). No 'free', 'trial', '30 days', 'no credit card', or MAU figures appear anywhere on the page; the 'START WITH ESSENTIALS' CTA promises nothing about cost or trial. (2) The 'Airship guarantees results' promo (https://www.airship.com/lp/airship-results-guarantee/, reinforced by CEO blog post 2026-10-21) is an enterprise outcomes/money-back-style commitment, NOT a free offer; terms are not public. (3) No startup program and no education/student license were found on the current site. (4) The Essentials registration page is JS-rendered, so trial duration/credit-card requirements could not be verified from static fetch; if a trial exists it is not advertised with published terms. CAVEAT: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted, so I could NOT independently verify the exact sunset date of the legacy Engage 100-MAU free tier or scan for 2025-2026 announcements via search; absence claims are based on reading the official pages today (high confidence for absence-of-mention on the checked pages, medium for 'definitively sunset' since a trial may exist inside the unrendered registration flow).
Airtable2025-2026 changes: (1) Q2 2025 — Airtable discontinued the paid AI add-on; AI credits are now bundled into all plans (Free gets 500 credits/editor/month; paid tiers 7,500+). (2) Q3 2025 — ProductCentral add-on discontinued; Enterprise Scale Portals pricing moved to 'contact sales'. (3) Sep 2026 — a new 'automations per base' cap of 50 was imposed on ALL plans, then reversed two days later into tiered caps: 75 (Free) / 100 (Team) / 150 (Business) / 200 (Enterprise Scale) — automation RUN limits unchanged. (4) Airtable became part of Bending Spoons in Sept 2026 (per community announcements and third-party trackers) — program terms may shift; all offers above re-verified against official pages Oct 2026. (5) Third-party claim that the free record limit was 'cut from 1,200 to 1,000 in Feb 2026' (hackceleration) is unverified and contradicts the official 1,000-record limit that has stood since the 2023 pricing revamp — treat as noise. (6) 'Airtable Classrooms' is no longer a listed product; the current education offer set is the 120-day .edu Team trial + free Student plan (6-24 months) + 50% Education/Non-profit Team discount, all applied through support.airtable.com/articles/4920135568. Non-profit 50% Team discount exists in parallel but was out of scope (not a student/startup offer).
Airwallex2025-2026 changes: (1) PRICING RESTRUCTURE (Jul 2025): Airwallex moved to per-user pricing (Explore $0/user/mo, Grow $12/user/mo + platform fee, Accelerate custom); customers who signed up before June 1, 2025 were placed into fee-free Explore as of July 1, 2025. The older market-conditional 'free Explore' thresholds (AU A$5K/mo deposit or A$10K balance, else A$29; UK £10K, else £19) still appear for AU/UK/CA, while the US page now markets Explore as flat $0 with no minimum — the task's known fact ('$0 if deposit ≥$10K/mo or hold ≥$10K') reflects the older threshold model, now superseded on the US page. (2) STARTUP PROGRAM REFRAMED (29 Oct 2025): the global 'Airwallex for Startups' program was relaunched in Singapore with three pillars (stage-appropriate infrastructure, CFO Expert Panel, AI Sandbox) before expanding to the US/UK; the prior version's 'win a $10,000 local-currency business acceleration grant in select regions' was replaced by the current per-stage perk structure (US: $150K fee credits etc.). US program T&C last updated Sept 9, 2026, application window open through 31 Dec 2027. (3) LATITUDE 37 (30 Apr 2026): new A$100K equity-free grant for under-25 Australian AI founders — applications opened late May 2026, likely closed by today's date. (4) UoM partnership extended 24 Apr 2026 with a further ~A$2M; its 'Opportunity Fund' bursary has reached funding capacity and is closed. No Airwallex free offer for students was sunset, because none ever existed as a public product tier.
Aiven Free Plan2025-2026 changes that materially altered the free landscape: (1) 15 May 2025 — free-plan storage for PostgreSQL and MySQL reduced from 5GB to 1GB for all new and existing free services (changelog aiven.io/changelog/6ad6c429-6c1c-4418-abfb-2ca82f927414); no data loss, connections unchanged, but hobbyists needing more must move to paid 'hobbyist/Developer' plans. (2) Nov 12 / Dec 10 2025 — a new $5/month Developer tier launched for PostgreSQL and MySQL (1 CPU, 1GB RAM, 8GB storage, Basic support, services never auto-power-off, region area but not cloud selection); one changelog dated Nov 12 2025 says the Postgres Developer plan starts 'at $8 USD' while the Aiven blog states $5 — treat $5/month as the advertised starting price and verify in-console. (3) Dec 10 2025 — the Kafka free tier itself is new (previously Kafka had no $0 plan); before that date the free tier was PostgreSQL/MySQL/OpenSearch/Valkey only. (4) The startup program rebranded from 'Aiven Cluster Startup Program' to 'Aiven for Startups' (revamp in Aug 2023: age cap 5→7 years, funding cap Series A→B, new $12K non-partner tier; current form is aiven.io/startups). (5) One-off prize competitions, not recurring offers: $5,000 Free Kafka competition (closed 31 Jan 2026) and $1,000 Free Tier competition (closed 17 Apr 2026, winner announced 27 Apr 2026). Nothing was shut down in this window; the only reductions were the 2025 free-tier storage cut and Kafka's free-service-per-org limit (1).
Aiven Startup ProgramNo free offer was sunset in 2025–2026. The aiven.io/education URL 404s (no dedicated education/.edu license has ever been offered; students use the general Free Tier). A dedicated /startup-program and /blog/aiven-startup-program URLs 404; the live startup program lives at aiven.io/startups and is currently active (Typeform applications open). WebSearch budget was exhausted this session, so 2025–2026 change claims could not be verified via search; findings above were read directly from the live official pages (aiven.io/free-tier, aiven.io/pricing, aiven.io/developer-tier, aiven.io/startups) today, so current-state detail is high-confidence. The $300 new-account credit figure and the 30-day trial end-of-trial behavior (auto-charge vs downgrade vs data deletion) are not fully corroborated on the official pages — verify in-console before relying on them.
Akamai Linode2025 changes (verified against live pages on 2026-10-22): (1) Rebrand — Linode is now 'Akamai Cloud' following Akamai's 2025 acquisition of Linode; linode.com/pricing (and the whole marketing site) now 301-redirects to akamai.com/cloud, and signups still run through login.linode.com. The old standalone 'Linode $100/30-day trial' link (linode.com/trial) now 404s — the trial is now the 'US$100 in credits' promo on Akamai Cloud pages, with the FAQ example stating a 60-day window (older promos were 30 days; ~30-60 days is the right mental model, but the exact window is per your signup/confirmation). (2) No perpetual free VPS tier has ever existed at Linode — the only recurring 'free' element on the pricing page is Object Storage's monthly free outbound egress allowance before overage (quantity not quantified on the page). (3) learn.linode.com (Linode Academy) no longer resolves (DNS ENOTFOUND) — training content moved to training.akamai.com/learn; there is no student/educator free-hosting license at Akamai Cloud today, so any 2025/2026 source claiming a 'Linode free student plan' is outdated. (4) WebSearch was unavailable in this session (budget exhausted), so corroboration relied on official pages fetched today; the $100 offer's exact day count remains a FAQ example rather than a hard-published policy figure.
Algolia Free Plan2025-2026 changes: On October 2, 2025 Algolia launched the Grow Plus plan, bringing AI search capabilities (AI Synonyms, AI Ranking, Advanced Personalization, Query Categorization, Collections, 90-day analytics retention) to the self-serve/pay-as-you-go lineup at $1.75/1K requests - a material expansion of the paid tiers; Grow plan terms were last updated June 30, 2025 (previous version Sept 25, 2024). No core free offer was sunset or reduced in 2025-2026: the Free plan still lists 10K requests / 50K records / 5K recommend / 5K crawls with no credit card required, matching the known figures. There is no dedicated student/education license: /education and /for-education both return 404 and the pricing page exposes no student URL; the only education-adjacent item is 'Algolia Academy' resources offered as a perk inside the Startup program. One unresolved on-page discrepancy in the Startup program: the main block says <7 years old and <US$15M funding, while its FAQ says <3 years old and <US$5M funding. Note: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted, so the startup/education verification relied on direct WebFetch of official Algolia pages (fetched 2026-10-22), which is the intended high-confidence path.
AnthropicAs of 2026-10-22, confirmed today on official pages: (1) Claude Startups was EXPANDED on Oct 6, 2026 (free year of Claude Team up to 5 Premium seats + $1,000 one-time API credit + $45K Startup Stack + up to $100K via partner VCs), but within days (~Oct 9–13) Anthropic PAUSED the free Team/credit offers after ~21x expected applications ('currently over capacity'; all applications re-reviewed), and the official page now (Oct 22) says 'Our current cohort is now full, and we're not accepting new applications right now' — the program continues for existing members; the benefit line items were dropped from the live page. Next cohort is TBA (newsletter signup is the only official channel). (2) The original Claude Startups invite-only (partner-VC-link) model of early 2026 was replaced by a direct self-serve form between May 21–31, 2026; older guides describing the VC-link flow are outdated. (3) Claude for Open Source criteria were overhauled July 2026: the 5,000+ stars / 1M+ npm-downloads gate no longer applies, replaced by the six usage/maintainer thresholds now on the official page. (4) Claude Campus is closed for Fall 2026 (application window Sep 1–12, 2026; program runs Sep 2026–Jun 2027; next cycle not yet announced). (5) Claude for Teachers: sign-up deadline June 30, 2027 for the free-year. (6) Nothing was sunset for the consumer Free plan or the API docs' 'small free credits to test the API.' General credit terms (secondary): promotional credits expire one calendar year from issuance; the Oct 2026 startup $1,000 credit was reported to expire six months after grant — the two sources conflict, re-verify per-award. Also note: Anthropic no longer maintains a public 'startup program official terms' page beyond the May 22, 2025 version; the active program now lives under claude.com/programs/* and the domain rebranded anthropic.com → claude.com (301 redirects), so some archived anthropic.com URLs now 404 or redirect. Research caveat: the session's web-search budget was exhausted mid-run; all primary claims above were verified by direct fetch of official pages today (claude.com/pricing, claude.com/programs/startups, /campus, /team-plan-for-scientists, /frontier-academy, /contact-sales/claude-for-oss, claude.com/solutions/education and /teachers, support.claude.com article 11199177, anthropic.com/news/ai-for-science-program, platform.claude.com/docs pricing); items marked medium/low come from the klymentiev.com tracker (checked 2026-09-25) and Oct 2026 press (TechCrunch, CNBC, 9to5AI, Times of India) or the fund's own page (Aug 2026).
AnthropicAs of 2026-10-22 the Claude for Startups program has changed materially from its 2024-2025 form: it is now run in cohorts, and the current cohort is FULL with no new applications accepted, so the $45K/$100K credits are only claimable when the next cohort opens (newsletter sign-up is the only current action). No program is sunset, but two notes: (1) The Claude API no longer lists a standalone free trial-credit offer for new accounts on the official pricing page — free API usage is only via the startup credits or the Team plan for scientists, so any historical small free API trial should not be assumed. (2) Two genuinely free education-side offers DO exist today and are the effective 'education' equivalents of a free tier: the Claude Team plan for scientists (standard seats $0 for 12 months for accredited-university/nonprofit PIs, then renews at paid price) and Claude for Teachers (free premium access for US K-12 educators, sign up by June 30, 2027 for the free year). A university-wide 'education license' exists but is a paid discounted institutional plan, not a free self-serve student offer. WebSearch budget was exhausted this session, so claims above were verified by reading the official pages directly today (claude.com/programs/startups, /pricing, /solutions/education, /programs/team-plan-for-scientists, /solutions/teachers); figures like the $45K/$100K amounts and the $0 scientist pricing were confirmed on the pages, but I could not cross-check third-party news coverage of 2025-2026 changes.
AppsmithChanges and gaps as of 2026-10-22. (1) Jan 2025 pricing overhaul (announced in Launch Week, Jan 12, 2025): moved from usage-based pricing (tracker cites $0.40/hour) to Business at $15/user/month; Free capped at 5 cloud users (previously unlimited) and 5 workspaces (new quota); Business capped at 99 users (previously unlimited); Enterprise $2,500/month for 100 users. Sources: https://www.appsmith.com/blog/appsmith-launch-week-1 and https://pricingsaas.com/companies/appsmith/diffs/2025Q1. (2) GitHub SSO: the tracker says it was removed from Free in 2025, but the official page shows both 'Google SSO' (bullet) and 'Google and GitHub OAuth' (table). Verify before relying on GitHub login on Free. (3) The brief's '10 apps' Free limit is not on Appsmith's official pricing; it matches ToolJet's free cloud plan. (4) Third-party comparison pages dated Aug to Oct 2026 (Softwr, 30 Aug 2026; Taskade, Sep 2026; Jetadmin, 26 Sep 2026; Awesomic, 8 Oct 2026) show the same Free (5 cloud users, 5 workspaces, 3 Git repos) and Business ($15/user/month) terms, so the plan terms look unchanged through October 2026 (medium confidence, secondary sources). (5) No education, student, teacher or academic license found, so no education finding is listed. Checked: the pricing page; appsmith.com/industry/education (only 'Start building for free' and sales@appsmith.com); the docs intro; the homepage; and the GitHub Student Developer Pack page (Appsmith not found there; the fetch may not have shown every offer). Institutions are routed to sales@appsmith.com. (6) Startup program: no end date or acceptance timeline is stated; aggregator record dates (Sept to Oct 2026) are not program start dates. (7) The Business Cloud 30-day promo is from June 2025, time-limited, and its current status is unconfirmed. (8) Search gap: the web search budget ran out during this run, so I could not search for other 2026 announcements, other startup or education programs, or the current status of the Business Cloud promo. Official pages were fetched directly instead. To run those searches, raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION and rerun.
Autodesk Education2025–2026 changes confirmed from official pages read today: (1) Single Sign-On mandate — Autodesk Education accounts will be required to use SSO (school credentials) starting August 7, 2026; schools like SMU enabled it in Aug 2025 (blog.smu.edu, 2025-08-07). (2) Multi-seat sunset — the US Education products page states 'Starting December 31, 2027, Autodesk will no longer offer new network and multi-seat licenses' and pushes institutions to Education institution subscriptions; the Asia-Pacific page (dated May 2025) states the cutoff as March 2026 for that region — i.e., the network/multi-seat lab-license model is being rolled off region by region starting 2026, so multi-seat classroom plans should be migrated now. (3) Startup program — status unverifiable as of 2026-10-22 (403 fetch blocks + exhausted shared web-search budget); no active free startup offer appeared in results. (4) No evidence today that the core 1-year free education license for students/educators changed, was reduced, or sunset — it remains 1-year, renewable, 3-device, full-feature. (5) Consumer free tiers (e.g., Fusion personal-use, AutoCAD Web basic) were NOT re-verified today and should be treated as unconfirmed; if they were sunset, that would land here after re-verification.
AWS (Amazon Web Services)SUNSET / MATERIAL CHANGES 2024-2026: (1) The classic AWS 12-month free tier (EC2 750 hrs/mo, S3 5 GB, 1M input + 1M output free Bedrock model-invocation tokens per month for 12 months across ~75 models, RDS 750 hrs, etc.) has been SUPERSEDED by the new Free plan / Paid plan model: $100 signup credits + up to $100 earned, 6-month window, 30+ 'always free' services, and notably no payment method required for most new customers. The Bedrock-specific free-token docs page (docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/free-trial.html) now renders empty/removed, and the new Free Tier FAQ contains zero mention of Bedrock free tokens, 750 EC2 hours, or 5 GB S3 - legacy per-service allowances no longer exist. So the 'free Bedrock tokens for students' front is effectively dead; Bedrock access now comes via (a) Free plan $100-200 credits, (b) AWS Activate credits, or (c) Kiro credits for agentic coding. (2) June 2024: AWS doubled the portfolio/accelerate credit ceiling from $100,000 to $200,000 (announced July 1, 2024, for startups that raised Series A in the past 12 months; seed stage stayed at $100,000 at the time; credits' expiry moved from 1 year to 3 years - per CNBC 2024-06-28). The current credits page (2026) shows one Portfolio tier at up to $200,000. (3) 2026: the credits page was redesigned (awslabs/startups PR #75, June 2026) renaming tiers to Activate Founders ($1,000 start, up to $5,000 - NOT the ~$100 sometimes misreported; the $100 figure matches the new Free plan signup credit, not Activate), Activate Portfolio (up to $200,000), and adding the invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier ($200,000+) plus the new Kiro Startup Credits program. (4) 'Known so far' figures from the task (~$100K Portfolio, ~$100 Founders) are outdated: Portfolio is now up to $200K and Founders is $1,000-to-start/up-to-$5,000. No standalone student Bedrock/LLM free offer from AWS exists today - the closest student fronts are the no-card Kiro Student Offer (1,000 credits/month x 12 months) and AWS Academy (training only, no credits). Verification caveat: today's live fetches of aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/ and free sub-pages intermittently returned 403 via proxy, but credits-page content was captured today via the official page mirror in the awslabs/startups repo and search snippets of the live page; free-plan figures came directly from aws.amazon.com/free/ and /free/free-tier-faqs/ read today.</sunset_notes>
AWS (Amazon Web Services)Major 2025-2026 changes (all verified on official AWS pages fetched today, 2026-10-22; WebSearch budget was exhausted so only official-page fetches were used): (1) The legacy usage-based 12-month AWS Free Tier (750 RDS hours, 5 GB S3, etc.) was replaced by a credit-based model for accounts created on/after July 15, 2025 — $100 at sign-up + up to $100 earned, Free plan (6-month auto-closing account) vs Paid plan (12-month credit validity); a refreshed sign-up experience launched October 2026, and pre-October accounts still see the old credits console. The RDS pricing page still documents the legacy 750-hour/20 GB free database for pre-July-15-2025 accounts only. (2) Per-service 'Always Free' and legacy free-trial figures (e.g., Athena GB-per-month, Redshift RA3/RA2 1-node 12-month developer tier and 30-day standard tier, Glue 1M source-records ETL free allowance, Bedrock character-based free tiers) no longer appear on today's service pricing pages — the credit model absorbed them, so do not quote legacy numbers for new accounts. (3) Activate program structure is now three tiers: Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000), Portfolio (up to $200,000 via provider Org ID), and an invite-only 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' tier adding $200,000+ for AI startups scaling past the Portfolio ceiling. Credits now explicitly cover Amazon Bedrock third-party models (OpenAI, Mistral, Anthropic, Meta). Note a discrepancy: /free/offers/ still lists Activate as '$1,000 to $100,000' — likely stale vs the $200,000 ceiling on the credits page/FAQ. (4) New Kiro startup credits program (up to a year of Kiro Pro+, 2/10/30-user tiers, 'limited time only', excludes France/Germany/Italy/Spain/Poland/UAE and ~13 other countries). (5) AWS Academy / Cloud Career Academies: no working page found on today's main AWS site (URLs 404; cloudcareeracademies.org DNS-failed) — status unconfirmed; AWS Educate (free, 13+, no credits/vouchers) is explicitly still active with a 2026 copyright. Also of note: AWS Educate bundles no cloud credits and no free exam vouchers on any page I could read today.
AWS (Amazon Web Services)(1) AWS Free Tier revamp: the classic structure - 12 months of Free Trial on ~75 services plus a separate 'Always Free' tier - is no longer on the official page; it has been replaced (2025-2026) by the Free plan / Paid plan model with up to $200 in credits, 90+ services for up to 6 months, and '30+ services always free within monthly usage limits'. The exact launch date of the revamp could not be confirmed (web-search budget exhausted mid-session); the page read today (2026-10-22) shows only the new structure. (2) AWS Activate: the legacy 'standard program up to $1,000 in credits' copy no longer appears on the live portal; the join page now says 'Apply for up to $200,000 in Activate Credits' while the application widget caps at 'up to $100,000' - consistent with a headline increase beyond the old $100K Accelerate ceiling, unconfirmed via a dated press release. (3) AWS Impact Accelerator, the US-only predecessor (part of AWS's 2022 $30M commitment to Black, Women, Latino and LGBTQIA+ founders), was superseded by the global AWS Impact Bootcamps announced Jul 30, 2025; the 2022-2023 US cohorts are closed. (4) All AWS Impact Bootcamps application windows through the 2026 Tokyo and Berlin cohorts are closed (deadlines Feb 27 and Mar 13, 2026), so as of 2026-10-22 the $5,000 credits, $50K grant and $15K credits are only obtainable in future, not-yet-announced cohorts. (5) Research caveat: https://startups.aws.com/lp/aws-impact-bootcamps currently returns HTTP 500 at the server level (its Next.js content payload is intact and was extracted from the raw HTML); the shared WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so 'was this changed in 2025-2026' checks rely on today's live pages rather than dated announcements.
AWS (Amazon Web Services)Key 2025-2026 changes: (1) 2025-07-15 cutover - AWS replaced the legacy 12-month service-level Free Tier with a credit-based model: new accounts get a 6-month 'Free plan' ($100 signup credits + up to $100 service-exploration credits = up to $200) or a Paid plan; accounts created before 2025-07-15 are grandfathered on the old 12-month per-service free tier (e.g. RDS: 750 hours/month single-AZ, 20 GB gp2 storage, 20 GB backup). (2) Free-plan accounts auto-close at 6 months or when credits run out; data retained 90 days, then permanently erased. (3) Startup program restructuring: the /startups/credits/ page now lists 'Activate Founders' (up to $5,000; starts at $1,000) and 'Activate Portfolio' (up to $200,000, was the $150,000 Founders' Program era ceiling; the previous 'Accelerate' up to $100,000 tier name no longer appears) plus two newer tracks - 'AWS Credits for AI Startups' ($200,000+, invite-only post-Portfolio) and 'Kiro Startup Credits'. The exact 2025-2026 rename/retirement dates could not be confirmed: WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted, so only the current official pages could be verified. (4) No .edu-gated student dollar-credit license was found: the education front is AWS Educate (free training/badges, no credits) and institutional 'special pricing' programs; AWS Academy pages returned 404 on fetched sub-paths, so its current status/offerings are unverified rather than confirmed active or sunsetting. (5) Free-plan credits are unavailable in China regions or AWS GovCloud (US); the Free plan also excludes all promotional credits (Activate), so startup credits effectively require a Paid-plan account.
AWS (Amazon Web Services)Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 - the classic 12-month per-service Free Tier was replaced for NEW accounts with a credit-based model: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned via 5 console activities, a 6-month Free plan window (account closes at 6 months or when credits run out), 90+ services, and 30+ always-free services; accounts created before July 15, 2025 retain the legacy 12-month tier. Free-tier-eligible EC2 instances changed too (t2.micro dropped; t3.small, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large added). (2) Short-term free trials are now paid-plan-only; the Free plan only gets 'Always Free' offers. (3) Feb 21, 2023 - AWS Educate stopped its old monetary grants ($35/$100 student grants, $75/$200 educator grants); educator tooling moved to AWS Academy. (4) Feb 16, 2026 - Free plan (Free Tier) accounts became ineligible for promotional credits, which affects AWS Cloud Credit for Research and similar programs (a Paid plan is required to hold awards). (5) Aug 20, 2026 - AWS launched Student Rewards on AWS Builder Center as part of a $500M+ student commitment; the old 'AWS Activate for Students' style offer no longer appears on aws.amazon.com - student benefits are now Builder Center Student Rewards + AWS Educate + AWS Academy. (6) October 2026 - new AWS sign-up experience launched; credit-expiry display moved to the Settings Portal (accounts that signed up before Oct 2026 still use the Credits page in the Billing console). No free offer listed above was fully sunset as of 2026-10-22.
AWS (Amazon Web Services)Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) July 15, 2025 — AWS replaced the long-standing 12-month Free Tier (750 hrs/mo t2.micro/t3.micro + 30 GB EBS + 100 GB egress, 30+ always-free services) with a credit-based model for NEW accounts: $100 sign-up credits + up to $100 earned = up to $200, with a 6-month no-cost Free plan that auto-closes. Legacy 12-month benefits remain ONLY for accounts created before July 15, 2025. Also, since the revamp, a credit card/payment method is no longer required to sign up for most new customers (previously mandatory). Note: CNBC's June 28, 2024 report that Series A startups got $200K from July 1, 2024 was NOT reflected in 2025 official materials (which still said 'up to $100,000'); the cap only officially moved to $200K in June 2026. AWS (Amazon Web Services)Sunset/changed in 2025-2026: (1) 15 Jul 2025 (announced AWS Summit NY, 15-16 Jul 2025): the 12-month Free Tier was retired for NEW accounts and replaced by the credit-based Free Plan ($100 signup credit + up to $100 explore credits, 6-month auto-closing plan, 90-day post-expiry data retention); pre-cutoff accounts keep their legacy 12-month offers until each one's own expiry. (2) EC2 free-VPS offer sunset: t2.micro (and hours-based t3.micro 750 h/mo) free tiers no longer apply to new accounts; new free-tier-eligible EC2 types are t3.micro, t3.small, t4g.micro, t4g.small, c7i-flex.large, m7i-flex.large, consumed via credits. (3) AWS Activate was folded into AWS for Startups: /aws.amazon.com/activate/ now serves the startups program (startups.aws.com); headline credit figure is 'up to $200,000' with published tiers $1,000-$100,000, 80+ partner offers valued up to $800,000, plus undisclosed extra AI-startup credits and Kiro credits — replacing older multi-year $1M total caps and old student/faculty Activate tiers (up to $100k/$150k) which no longer appear on official pages. (4) The Free Plan explicitly cannot combine with any other promotional/incentive offer, and joining AWS Organizations/Control Tower auto-upgrades to Paid and voids remaining explore credits. (5) AWS signals a new sign-up experience in October 2026 (FAQ note); Free Tier Terms last updated 9 Jul 2025. AWS (Amazon Web Services)Material 2025-2026 changes (all verified against AWS pages today, 2026-10-22): (1) Jul 15, 2025 — the legacy "12 months free" tier was replaced for NEW accounts by a credit-based Free Tier: $100 sign-up credit + up to $100 earned, 6-month Free plan; every legacy 12-month offer on pre-Jul-15-2025 accounts (including legacy CloudWatch 12-month items) has now fully lapsed. (2) Oct 2026 — AWS launched a new sign-up experience: social sign-in (Google/GitHub/Apple/Amazon), no credit card needed for most customers, and credit tracking moved from the Billing console to a Settings Portal for accounts signed up after the rollout; the Free Tier FAQ now states base credits expire 6 months (vs. 12 months in the Jul 9, 2025 Terms) — a live contradiction AWS hasn't harmonized, so treat 6 months as the safe planning horizon. (3) Dec 3, 2025 — Kiro Startup Credits v1 launched (up to 100 users, pre-seed to Series B, apply by Dec 31, 2025) and closed; relaunched Apr 7, 2026 with 2/10/30-user tiers, early-stage to Series A (VC-backed per the current promotion), apply by Dec 31, 2026; startups with active AWS Activate credits are excluded from the Kiro promo. (4) Jun 2024 (context) — AWS doubled Portfolio credits $100K to $200K and extended expiry 1 year to 3 years; still the structure in effect. (5) Feb 2023 (context) — AWS Educate stopped issuing universal/institutional credit grants; it is now free learning only, and student credits now flow through Builder Center Student Rewards (up to $579 incl. $30 credits + $100 exam voucher, launched Aug 2026). (6) CloudFront's classic "100 GB/month always-free" line is now presented as a standing $0 "Free" plan tier with 100 GB + 1M requests + 5 GB S3 storage credits and no overage charges — not sunset, but restructured. No CloudWatch/observability offer was sunset; the known limits (10 metrics, 1M API requests, 5 GB logs, 100 canaries, 3 dashboards, 10 alarms, 100K X-Ray traces) all still apply as ALWAYS-FREE allowances. There is no CloudWatch-specific startup or student grant beyond the general AWS Free Tier credits and Activate credits, which can offset CloudWatch usage on a Paid plan.

The stale /free/offers page still shows old $1,000-$100,000 figures and 7-10 day processing, inconsistent with the current credits page ($5,000/$200,000, 5-10 days) — treat the credits page as authoritative. AWS (Amazon Web Services)1) Amazon Pinpoint sunset (HARD DATE): AWS ends support for Amazon Pinpoint on October 30, 2026 — console access and all Pinpoint resources (endpoints, segments, campaigns, journeys, analytics) terminate that day; only the messaging-channel APIs (SMS, voice, mobile push, OTP, phone number validate) continue under the rebranded "AWS End User Messaging" service. Anyone relying on Pinpoint journeys/campaigns must migrate before 2026-10-30. 2) Legacy per-service Free Tier replaced: the old model — e.g., "SES: 3,000 emails/month free for the first 12 months, then $0.10/1K" and "SNS: 100 SMS/month free, US regions" — is no longer published; as of the October 2026 sign-up experience, new accounts instead get up to $200 in Free Tier credits over 6 months (Free plan auto-closes at 6 months or when credits run out). The "then $0.10/1K" figure survives, but as the à-la-carte SES price, while NEW SES accounts default to the more expensive Essentials plan ($0.16/1K) starting July 21, 2026. 3) The formerly permanent "3,000 emails/month free (US regions, no purchase of sending capacity)" SES allowance is not listed on today's official pricing page — treat it as superseded by the credit model for new accounts; legacy account behavior not verifiable (docs pages didn't render). 4) The legacy 100-free-SMS/month US-regions tier also no longer appears on End User Messaging pricing; SMS is now fully pay-per-message. 5) AWS Activate credit ceiling was raised to $200,000 (some pages still show the old $100,000 figure — a 2025-2026 program change, not a sunset).

(3) AWS Activate restructured in 2025-2026: old tiers (Starter $5k / Portfolio up to $100k / YC up to $250k / $5k-$100k sub-tiers) are no longer publicly listed; current structure is Founders ($1,000, up to $5,000 for select participants) + Portfolio (up to $200,000, cap raised from $100,000), with eligibility shifted from 'funded up to Series A' to 'pre-Series B, most recent funding round within the last 12 months', and the application flow now goes through the AWS Builder ID. The only university-scoped free offer is AWS Academy (free institution participation + exam vouchers). (6) AWS Educate availability excludes China and Switzerland and raises minimum age in the EU (16) and Algeria/Lebanon/Portugal (18).

Billing is monthly. Subscriptions are managed through GitHub Sponsors, which requires a GitHub login. The FAQ says higher tiers can also pay by invoice, ACH, wire or check (which tiers is not stated). The FAQ does not state whether a credit card is required. PR 7341 (merged Nov 14, 2025) requires 2 maintainer approvals and signed tags for each release. This affects support expectations, not pricing. (b) Jan 29, 2026: Railway became a Caddy sponsor. Railway's promotions on the Caddy site are Railway's own offers, not Caddy's. (c) Feb 2026: ZeroSSL, the executive sponsor that owns the Caddy trademark, was acquired by HID Global (ASSA ABLOY), per Matt Holt's Feb 5, 2026 forum post. (d) Price snapshots disagree: an older search-index copy of the GitHub Sponsors page showed Business at $2,999 and Business+ at $5,900. The official site and today's GitHub Sponsors page show Business $999, Business+ $2,999, Enterprise $5,900 and Enterprise+ $11,900. The change date is unverified; the official site governs. (e) Aug 11, 2025: a forum announcement says Caddy took part in the GitHub Secure Open Source Program 2025. That is a project-level program, not a student or startup offer; details were not fetched.Not offers, or wrong: the hosted demo at caddydemo subdomains is 'intentionally restricted' and is not a trial. History/sunset: Chroma Cloud launched 2025-08-18 (changelog 'Introducing Chroma Cloud'), superseding the earlier Chroma Cloud trial; third-party tracker AgentDeals records a pricing-model change (discovered 2026-09-07, effective date unknown) from a legacy free tier (reported ~1M embeddings, 1 collection, 10K queries/day — unverified) to the current $0 Starter + $5 credits + metered pricing; the current metered terms were confirmed on the official pricing page today, so no free offer was sunsetting recently. Beware name confusion: 'Chroma Awards' (chromaawards.com), a film/music/games competition that recruited universities and students in 2025 with 'starter packs', internships and a $3M+ credit pool, is a separate organization, not the ChromaDB vendor. Course-sponsored credits (e.g., $250 Chroma Cloud credits for students of Hugo Bowne-Anderson's 'Building AI Applications' masterclass, Nov 2025 and Mar 2026 cohorts) are time-limited, sponsor-funded, cohort-specific — not a Chroma program.
CircleCI2025–2026 changes and absences: (1) No free trial credits on paid plans currently exist — the pricing page (read 2026-10-22) lists no trial duration, trial credit pool, or CC-for-trial offer, and an independent 2026-10-14 tracker (PulseSignal) records "Free trial: None listed"; the trial-circleci model has been superseded by the permanent 30,000-credit Free plan. (2) CircleCI Academy (free courses/certificate, formerly the closest thing to an education program) has been sunset — the support collection now says "CircleCI Academy is no longer available"; only SCORM downloads of the 17 former courses remain; no end date was published, but it was already gone by 2026-10-22. (3) Effective 2025-12-01, CircleCI changed unregistered-user billing: every unregistered user now counts individually toward the monthly active-user total (previously all unregistered users counted as one); the Free plan keeps 5 included active users at the same price, and OSS-enabled projects are exempt (unregistered OSS PR authors don't count as seats). (4) The Free plan's 30,000-credit monthly allowance has been stable through 2025–2026 (no observed change; PriceVersion shows $0/30,000 credits recorded as current from 2026-10-04); the 400k OSS Linux pool and 30k macOS/Windows OSS pool are also still live per the official open-source page, though the support doc (May 2026) still lists macOS OSS at 25,000 credits — a documentation inconsistency worth confirming with CircleCI support. (5) Related non-offer sunsets: Bitbucket Data Center support retired 2026-10-01 and Self-Hosted Runner Bitbucket Cloud retires 2026-11-12 (VCS deprecations, not pricing); Xcode image retention is rolling off older versions each month.

CloudflareChanges in 2025–2026: (1) The Cloudflare for Startups program was restructured from the April 2025 four-tier layout ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k, per blog post 'Startup Program update') to the current three-tier layout ($10k/$100k/$350k) — Tier 3 raised $5k→$10k and top tier $250k→$350k; regional pages (e.g. cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups) still display the outdated $250k/4-tier structure, so use the US .com page as authoritative. (2) Sept 22, 2025 (Birthday Week 2025): Students program launched (12 months free Workers Paid for US .edu 18+) and the program was extended to non-profits/civil-society orgs (up to $250k credits). (3) Sept 2025: 'Come build with us' hubs — program participants get office access in SF/Austin/London/Lisbon from Jan 2026. (4) Sept 28, 2026: 'The Cold Start' competition announced — 5 early-stage companies pitch at Cloudflare Connect; grand prize $500,000 in credits (competition, not a standing program). (5) Workers AI pricing page (last updated Oct 20, 2026) moved to per-model granular unit-based pricing display while backend billing remains Neuron-based; 8 frontier models are now gated behind a paid billing method. Sunset of 2022–2024 programs: the original $1.25B/$2B-pledge Workers Launchpad funding program (2022) evolved into the current cohort Launchpad; no free-offer sunsets this cycle — all five offers above remain active as of 2026-10-22. Cloudflare2025–2026 program changes: (1) The Startup Program was restructured in 2025 from the April 2025 4-tier structure ($5,000 / $25,000 / $100,000 / $250,000, 'founded within last 5 years') to the current 3 tiers ($10k / $100k / $350k, founded within last 10 years) — entry tier doubled, top tier raised $250k->$350k, and the $25k tier was dropped. Members on the old caps can request a case-by-case upgrade that does not reset their 12-month expiry. (2) Sept 22, 2025: program expanded to non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations (up to $250k credits; first application window ran until Dec 1, 2025). (3) Sept 22, 2025: Cloudflare for Students launched (12-month free paid developer features, US .edu only) — an education license/credit that did not exist before 2025. (4) 'The Cold Start' ($500k credit prize) closed with its Oct 19, 2026 Connect event; applications ended Oct 2, 2026. (5) Stale pages: some localized pages (e.g., cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups/) still show the old '5 years / $5k-$250k' criteria — treat as outdated; the global /startups/ page is authoritative. (6) Community note: a global Cloudflare outage on Nov 18, 2025 (Workers KV, Access, Turnstile, dashboard, core CDN) is a recurring single-vendor risk with free tiers. The pre-launch claim 'WAF (1,000 rules) on all [startup] tiers' is confirmed — but the regular Free plan only gets 5 WAF custom rules; 1,000 is the Enterprise-plan limit that the startup program includes at no cost.

(2) No trial credits — paid Workers plans simply start at $5/mo with usage-based overage; no free-trial-of-paid credits are offered. (3) No education license/.edu discount for infrastructure — cloudflare.com/plans (fetched 2026-10-22) shows usage-based Free/Paid tiers only and mentions no education, student, or nonprofit discount; no .edu email is required for any tier; the free tiers are universally available to any account. Cloudflare's only education-adjacent offering is free learning content/certification programs (Learning Hub / CF-ICD), which is training, not hosting/PaaS — and its landing page (developers.cloudflare.com/learning-hub) was 404, so status is unverified. 2025–2026 material changes affecting the free tiers: Pages is not sunset but is being pushed into a migrate-to-Workers posture (a 'Migrate From Pages' guide and a Pages index note steering users to Workers static assets; Pages limits unchanged: 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 20,000 files); Workers Logs free tier migrates to Cloudflare Observability pricing on 2026-12-01; Durable Objects SQLite storage billing begins January 2026; legacy KV API routes deprecated with 2026-10-15 migration deadline; R2 free tier (10 GB) unchanged, with new free sub-tiers added in 2026 for R2 SQL (10 GB scanned/mo) and R2 Data Catalog (1M ops + 10 GB compaction/mo), both billing-enabled 2026-08-03. Note: the WebSearch budget was exhausted for this session, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on official docs/changelog pages (fetched today) rather than press/news coverage; 'last updated' dates on the source pages are 2026, confirming the limits above are current.
Cloudflare2024-2026 changes: (1) Startup program was revamped Sep 2024 from a one-size $500k-era offer to a credits system capped at $250,000 (3 tiers). April 11, 2025: expanded to four tiers — $5,000 (bootstrapped/stealth, founded last 5 yrs), $25,000 (funded to $1M), $100,000 ($1M-$5M + approved VC/accelerator), $250,000 (Tier-1 VCs / AI / Workers Launchpad) — with a 5-year founding window and a 'BOOTSTRAPPED' promo-code path. The current 2026 page replaced this with three tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k), a 10-year founding window, 'funded up to Series B within the last 12 months', a hard payment-method-on-file requirement, and per-tier Workers AI caps. Old $250k/$5k-$250k four-tier copy still lingers on stale localized sub-pages (en-gb, en-ca) — treat cloudflare.com/startups as current. (2) Pages platform shift 2025-2026: Pages was consolidated into the Workers platform; docs now steer new projects to Workers ('Start new projects with Workers'; Pages Functions billed exactly as Workers), but Pages is not sunset — Free plan numbers stand as verified Sep 5, 2026 doc: $0, unlimited static requests/bandwidth, 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 100 custom domains/project, 20,000 files (raised from the prior 10,000 cap; paid tiers unlock 100,000 via PAGES_WRANGLER_MAJOR_VERSION=4), 25 MiB max asset. (3) No student/education program was created or sunset in this window; 'Project Cybersafe Schools' (free K-12 email security + DNS filtering, US districts) remains the only education-facing free offer. No trial-credit program (e.g., a $500 API-credit trial) is currently advertised on the official pages I read.
CloudflareMaterial 2025–2026 changes: (1) Startup Program tier structure was revamped repeatedly: Sept 2024 revamp set the ceiling at $250k; April 2025 expanded to 4 tiers ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k); by mid-2026 it was restructured into 3 tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k, per the current cloudflare.com/startups page and a May 2026 announcement). (2) Sep 2025: non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations became eligible for the Startup program (up to $250k) — application window opened with a Dec 1 close for that cohort. (3) Eligibility window widened from 'founded within last 5 years' (2019–2024) to 'founded within last 10 years.' (4) Workers free-plan subrequest limit changed Feb 2026: now 50 external + 1,000 Cloudflare-service subrequests per invocation (previously a flat 1,000 total). (5) Workers Logs: effective Dec 1, 2026 it moves to Cloudflare Observability pricing (a near-term change affecting the free observability front). (6) Sep 2026: Free-account creation expanded so users can create up to 5 standalone Free accounts via dashboard or API. No traditional 'education license discount' or separate individual developer-credit grant exists at Cloudflare; the free value for students is the perpetual Free plan + free Academy + discretionary Ambassador/Community grants, and for startups the credits program.
CloudflareMaterial 2025-2026 changes: (1) Startup program tiers were "recently updated" (undated on page, current as of 2026-10-22): Tier 3 raised $5,000→$10,000/yr and Tier 1 $250,000→$350,000/yr — older references to a "$5K credit" are stale; entry tier is now $10K. (2) No general-public time-boxed trial or one-off trial-credit offer exists; the only dollar-credit offer is the application-based startup program (perpetual free tiers are not trials and never expire). (3) New billable dimensions landed on the free platform in 2026: R2 SQL and R2 Data Catalog billing enabled 2026-08-03 for non-Enterprise accounts (free tier 10 GB scanned / 1M catalog ops / 10 GB compaction per month), AI Search billing begins 2026-11-01 (free: 5M ingestion tokens, 10 GB-month, 1K semantic + 1K full-text queries/month), and 2026-02-11 raised Workers subrequest limits (Free stays at 50 external/1,000 internal per invocation; Paid default 10,000, configurable to 10M) — free accounts can still accrue charges on metered add-ons, so set spend limits. (4) 2025-12-05 WAF max-payload change: Free zones already protected to 1 MB; paid plans need support ticket to enable. (5) Unbound/Bundled Workers plans are deprecated and unavailable to new accounts. CourseraMajor 2025 sunset: Coursera retired the classic unlimited 'audit mode' (full free access to lectures of most courses, no certificate) around Aug 8, 2025, replacing it with the Preview experience — free access is now capped at the FIRST module of most courses; 'Full Course, No Certificate' survived only for a select subset (470+ courses per Class Central's Aug 2026 audit, ~2% of the 21,000+ catalog). Consequences: (1) free access shrank from 'whole course minus certificate' to 'first module only' for the majority of courses; (2) the free-trial period for subscriptions is 7 days (not the 30 days some older guides cite) with a card captured up front; (3) certificates are explicitly withheld during free trials; (4) localized/geographic (geo) pricing was introduced in 2025, so paid upgrade prices vary by region while the free tiers stay free; (5) a 'Free Community Impact Courses' program (mental health, refugee support, public health) was added in 2025 as a partner-supported free-access carve-out; (6) no .edu-only student tier and no startup program exist as of 2026-10-22 (verified 404s on /startups, /plus, /for-universities; free paths are Preview, Full-Course-No-Certificate, financial aid, 7-day trials, and institution-provided Campus access). No free offer was closed in 2026 that had existed in 2025 beyond the audit->preview shrinkage; financial aid remains offered.

Data.govChanges verified 2025-2026 (read from official pages today; WebSearch budget was exhausted, so this reflects the official pages rather than news searches): (1) The catalog API was rebuilt: the new v4 API at api.gsa.gov/technology/datagov/v4 (keyed via api.data.gov) 'replaces the previous CKAN-based API' per resources.data.gov/catalog-api; the docs claim the older CKAN endpoint 'remains available in a read-only state for existing integrations', but probes to catalog.data.gov's former CKAN paths (/api/3/action/..., /api/3/, /api/2/, /dataset.json) all returned HTTP 404 today, so that read-only availability is uncertain or on a different host — low confidence, verify before relying on legacy CKAN integrations. (2) The legacy standalone Data.gov 'Developer Program' key portal keys.data.gov no longer resolves (DNS ENOTFOUND today), i.e. that older free-API-key program (zip codes, geographic names, transit routes style lookups) has been retired; equivalent agency APIs now live under api.data.gov. (3) No price changes: everything remains free; the OPEN Government Data Act remains the statutory basis. Caveat: the known-context line 'many datasets need no key' is confirmed for downloads/search, but API access to the v4 catalog API does require a (free) key; agency-specific egress/download costs never apply to data.gov itself.

(2) The 'National Data Hub' named in the task's known-so-far is a misnomer — no such API product appears in official documents; the program is the National Data Library (>£100M of DSIT's £1.9B; progress update 26 Jan 2026; spring-2026 detail milestone). (3) Related UK open-data sunsets: ONS PublishMyData API fully decommissioned 31 March 2025 (outage from 3 March 2025; data moved to ONS portals and the new ONS Data API / api.beta.ons.gov.uk/v1/search), and ONS retired its aggregated-search /data endpoints in favour of the ONS Search API. (4) The old ODI terms-and-conditions URL (data.gov.uk/terms-and-conditions) now 404s, so documented rate limits are gone — access remains free and keyless. (5) No free-tier, trial-credit, startup-program, or education-license offer existed or changed on data.gov.uk in 2025-2026: it was and is unconditionally free under the Open Government Licence, so there is nothing student/startup-specific to sunset.

MapboxVerdict: no genuine student or startup free offer found as of 2026-10-22, so findings is empty. The only free usage is Mapbox's standing pay-as-you-go monthly tier, which is not student- or startup-specific, plus lower card-free demo caps. The monthly tier resets each billing period and no expiry is stated. Full tiers come with standard pay-as-you-go billing, which requires a payment method (https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/faq/how-does-pay-as-you-go-billing-work/ ; https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/guides/signup/ says a card at sign-up "includes the generous Mapbox free tiers"). Monthly free amounts (https://www.mapbox.com/pricing): GL JS map loads 50,000; Maps SDKs for Mobile 25,000 MAU; Directions 100,000 requests; Temporary Geocoding 100,000 requests; Static Images 50,000; Vector Tiles 200,000; Raster Tiles 750,000. Permanent Geocoding has no free band. Overage: GL JS $5.00 per 1,000 loads (50,001-100,000); Directions $2.00 per 1,000 (100,001-500,000); Temporary Geocoding $0.75 per 1,000 (100,001-500,000). A GL JS map load is counted per map object initialized in a 12-hour session (signup guide), so recreating maps uses allowance faster. Card-free demo access (https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/guides/signup/ ; https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/guides/demo-access/) has much lower caps: GL JS 5,000 loads, Maps SDKs for Mobile 100 MAU, Directions 10,000, Temporary Geocoding 10,000, Static Images 5,000. It allows one token that cannot be created, rotated or deleted, blocks all APIs at a cap until a manual upgrade, cannot be switched back after upgrading, excludes Raster Tiles and Permanent Geocoding, and states no end date. Mapbox offers no monthly spending cap (https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/faq/can-i-set-up-a-cap-for-monthly-spending/). Usage past the free tier is invoiced to the card, and a failed charge risks deactivation. Student check: https://www.mapbox.com/education says sign-up is free and the free tiers "cover almost all student projects", with an educational-email sign-up instruction but no credits, bundles, verification rules or expiry. Startup check: https://www.mapbox.com/startups returns HTTP 404; the pricing page links only Nonprofit Support and Education; the account docs index (https://docs.mapbox.com/accounts/llms.txt) lists no student, startup, education or credit program. Adjacent, out of scope: https://www.mapbox.com/nonprofit offers donated or discounted services to nonprofits and positive-impact organizations, with no dollar figures; applications are reviewed in May and November, support starts in July or January, and renewal is annual. Conflict: freetier.wiki (https://freetier.wiki/services/mapbox/) lists "Card required: No"; the official pages above contradict that for the full tiers, so official pages were used. Confidence: high that no student or startup offer exists on the official Mapbox pages checked; medium overall, because only one of the two WebSearch calls ran (the other was refused by the budget) and its results showed no such program. If the index accepts general, eligibility-free tiers, the standing tier above is the entry to add, with eligibility "any account with a payment method on file"; that scoping is the orchestrator's call.

Microsoft Azure2025-2026 program changes (verified via press + official MS pages): (1) July 1, 2025 — Microsoft for Startups restructure: the Founders Hub four-tier model (up to $150,000 in Azure credits, applied for with a validated business plan) was RETIRED, as were the 'Founders Hub' label, the Expert Network, and the Champion Network. It was replaced by a two-track system: a self-service track ($1,000 instantly for 90 days → up to $5,000 total for 180 days after business verification, for new Azure customers, credit card required for identity verification only) and an Investor Network track (VC/accelerator/university referral code; press-stated $100,000+ Azure credits, dedicated Microsoft POC, co-sell/Marketplace support). Pegasus (invitation-only dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) was explicitly unchanged. Existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to pay-as-you-go; a Microsoft Q&A answer (July 2025) confirms non-investor applicants apply via the new 'Microsoft for Startups Credit Portal.' (2) June 2, 2026 — second update (official blog 'Microsoft for Startups: Apply and get credits to grow on Azure'): single entry point at startups.microsoft.com; startups 'start building immediately with Startup credits'; the up-to-$150,000 ceiling was RESTORED but now unlocked incrementally 'over time based on service adoption and sustained Azure usage and verified progress' rather than as a one-time grant; Investor Network partners unlock 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits' earlier; current eligibility caps: not more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits, no Series C funding or later, private for-profit, HQ in an Azure region, no crypto mining; applications reviewed in ~3 business days (learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview, page updated 2026-08-05). Already-installed software keeps working. This is why the '12 months of Visual Studio Pro' line from the known-so-far notes no longer maps to any current Azure student offer (Pro/Enterprise IDE now comes via GitHub Student Developer Pack or Visual Studio Dev Essentials Premium). NetlifyBig 2025-2026 change: the old usage-metered Free/Starter plan (100GB bandwidth, 300 build minutes, 125,000 function invocations, 1M edge invocations, 10GB storage, no card) is now a LEGACY plan. From 4 Sep 2025 all NEW accounts use credit-based plans (Free = 300 cr/mo). Existing legacy accounts keep their old plan but may switch to credit-based (irreversible). So the 'known' free tier was restructured from metered (100GB) to credit-based (300 credits ≈ ~15GB or ~20 deploys). Other timeline: 1 Oct 2025 credit-pack rebalance (Personal 500cr/$5, Pro 1500cr/$10; Pro monthly cut 5000→3000 credits); 17 Apr 2026 Pro = unlimited seats, form submissions made free on all credit plans, meter rates changed (bandwidth 10→20 cr/GB, compute 5→10 cr/GB-hr, web requests 3→2 cr/10k). No student/education program was launched or sunset; one never existed. Startup program is a VC-backed discount (referral-only) and is not a free-credit offer; its official /startups/ page returned 404 today, so specifics come from a third-party directory.

NominatimNo student, startup, trial-credit, or education program existed at OSMF/Nominatim, so none was sunset in 2025–2026. What DID materially change in 2025–2026: (1) the OSMF Nominatim Usage Policy was tightened — bulk scripts now hard-capped at 4 req/min (single machine, single thread, >1-day or recurring jobs), with repeated-identical-query clients classified 'faulty' and blockable; (2) a new 'Usage in LLMs' clause: LLMs may only suggest the service if they prominently surface the policy, and no-code/low-code/'vibe-coding' platforms are prohibited from embedding the public API as a generic geocoding feature — aimed squarely at AI-generated app stacks; (3) explicit ban on reselling/repackaging geocoding output, naming package/vehicle tracking and API resellers, and a warning that commercial users may have access revoked 'without notice'; (4) software releases: Nominatim 5.2.0 on 2025-10-29 (wiki, accessed 2026-09), and /search.php deprecated in favor of /search. Nothing was closed; the free public service remains live as of 2026-10-22 (policy page fetched today).

Cloud (hosted inference) models first launched in preview Sep 19, 2025; Team plan opened Aug 31, 2026. Ollama provides no free notebooks or free ML-training compute — its cloud is inference-only and the local runtime is for inference/serving; training/fine-tuning requires separate tooling.

Open-source route: Community on GitHub (free, apply through Support, OSI license, GitHub login). The 50% education and nonprofit discounts apply only to paid plans. TwilioMaterial 2025–2026 changes verified on official pages today (2026-10-22): (1) Twilio trial is now a hard 30-day window with product-specific free units (100 SMS / 100 WhatsApp / 3,000 emails / 75 voice minutes) instead of the older indefinite restrictions-only trial with no bundled units; accounts that don't upgrade inside 30 days expire, with a fresh post-upgrade free-unit bundle for eligible products. (2) No shared dollar trial credit exists anymore (the old 'trial credit' wording survives only as the vague 'small credit' on the startups page). (3) Twilio's Startups offering no longer includes startup credits or discounts — official FAQ: 'We do not offer additional credits to startups at this time'; it is now free educational resources + a sales-team channel. (4) SendGrid's permanent free email plan (400/day) has been gone since 2023; the current Email API 'free' offer is a 60-day trial (100 emails/day), with the ongoing free quota living on the Marketing Campaigns plan instead (2,000 contacts / 6,000 emails/month). (5) Twilio University (the former free student/educator bootcamp) is no longer reachable — /en-us/university returns 404 and the developer hub lists no student program; the education page is institution-sales-only, so no .edu-based free offer exists. (6) twilio.com/try-twilio now 307-redirects straight into the Auth0 signup flow (the trial marketing page was folded into signup); 'no credit card required' survives as pricing-page copy. Caveat: WebSearch was unavailable in this session (shared session search budget exhausted), so sunset claims rely on today's official-page fetches plus pre-existing knowledge; no 2025-26 press release or changelog entry could be independently re-searched. Ongoing paid costs after free units: SMS $0.0083–$0.014/msg, voice $0.0085/min inbound and $0.014/min outbound, Twilio Email $0.0013/email, Conversations $0.05/active user/month, TaskRouter $0.06/task, Functions $0.0001/invocation; phone-number rental and A2P 10DLC registration are paid-only.
Twilio SendGridMaterial 2025-2026 changes observed: (1) The Twilio trial was restructured from a shared dollar-credit model (historically the widely documented $10 trial balance) to per-product FREE UNITS (100 SMS / 100 WhatsApp / 3,000 emails / 75 voice minutes) — the current docs state units are given 'instead of a shared dollar balance' — and now has a hard 30-day expiry ('Trial accounts expire after 30 days'), replacing the previously open-ended trial-until-upgrade model. (2) SendGrid is rebranded under Twilio: sendgrid.com 301-redirects to twilio.com/en-us/sendgrid, and the master pricing page lists 'Twilio Email starts at $0.0013/email' (page marked current as of Aug 2026); SendGrid's 60-day trial and persistent free Marketing tier (2,000 contacts / 6,000 emails/mo) remain. (2) Vercel for Startups: new 'Flexible Commitment' credit terms apply to startups accepted on/after August 6, 2026 - the credit amount became partner-negotiated (up to a $30,000 max) rather than a fixed amount, with a 50%-of-monthly-credit cap on AI Gateway usage. (3) v0 plan lineup was rebranded - the '$20 Premium' plan referenced by older student-program marketing no longer exists; v0 is now Free / Plus ($30/user/mo, $30 monthly credits) / Business ($100/user/mo), so the exact value of the 1-year free v0 for Students offer is unpublished. (4) AI Accelerator credit pools scaled up between cohorts: $4M+ (2025) -> $6M+ (Feb 2026 cohort, apps due 2026-02-16) -> $8M+ on the current page. (5) v0 for Students launched Oct 2025 with 5 schools (Stanford, Harvard, MIT, Princeton, UC Berkeley) and has since expanded to 15; the .edu-only verification has not been loosened as of Jan 2026. (6) Vercel Remote Cache became free (changelog item, 2025-2026 era), adding to the Hobby free offering.

It is gated by an approved Vercel Startup Partner, so it is not open to every startup. It was changed, not sunset. The CloudRoute change note (6 Aug 2026, Flex Commit, 50% AI Gateway limit) matches Vercel's own FAQ text. Size: Vercel's text says 'up to $30,000', a partner-dependent ceiling; no per-startup amount was verified. Student offers: none verified. The official startups credits FAQ lists general educational perks but no student offer (fetched 2026-10-22). A dedicated web search for a Vercel student offer could not run because the session's web search budget was used up, so a student-specific Vercel offer remains unverified. Vercel's standard Hobby plan is free per SaaSTweaks, but it is a general plan, not a student or startup program. Vercel Open Source Program (not a student or startup program): Startup Perk List (reviewed 2026-10-16) says new applications are closed, and sources conflict on the amount ($3,600 over three years vs over 12 months). Verification method: the official FAQ was read via a narrower WebFetch after the first full fetch failed on output size. The legal terms page (vercel.com/legal/startup-program-terms), cited by East Bridge Global, returned 404, so the legal terms were not checked directly.

XataMajor 2025–2026 sunset/change: the permanent free hosted tier that students/startups relied on is GONE. Xata's original product was a hosted 'serverless data platform' (spreadsheet-style UI, REST/TypeScript data API, full-text search, file attachments over Postgres) that shipped a permanent free tier with 15 GB of storage, no cold starts and no pausing. Sunset timeline: (1) On January 10, 2025 Xata pulled the search and files APIs out of the free plan; (2) in May 2025 Xata announced a rebuilt product centered on vanilla, unmodified Postgres with copy-on-write branching and data anonymization (pgstream) — the old platform was briefly renamed 'Xata Lite' and now returns HTTP 410 Gone at lite.xata.io; (3) in late 2025 Xata open-sourced its core under Apache 2.0, and the permanent free hosted tier was replaced by 'self-host the open-source version' plus a 14-day Xata Cloud trial (with a reported $100 signup credit) and no permanent free cloud plan. Consequence: if you used the old Xata free tier for a managed database, it no longer exists — the only permanent-free option is self-hosted open source; the managed path is the 14-day trial then pay-as-you-go ($0.012/hr + $0.28/GB/mo) or BYOC (quote-based). Note: WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so verification relied on the official pricing page + pricing.md + n8n docs read today plus multiple corroborating 2026 third-party trackers; the $100 credit could not be confirmed on the live official page.
Y Combinator2025: YC reportedly REMOVED the public "YC Startup School Deals & Offers" page (per startcamp.com, Feb 2025). Startup deals moved behind sign-in at deals.ycombinator.com, so YC Startup School members no longer automatically get the old public AWS $5K (non-China) / $1,500+3K rebate (China) tier; that older startup-school AWS deal is effectively retired. 2025-2026 partner CREDITS CHANGES (all EXPANSIONS, no partner program was sunset): (a) Anthropic raised its YC offer from $30K to $500K, no equity; (b) OpenAI overhauled to $500K no-equity + optional $1.5M equity tranche, and offered $2M via uncapped SAFE to the entire Spring 2026 batch (May 2026); (c) Google Cloud up to $500K + early Gemini access; (d) YC's apply-page headline raised to "$12M+ in free credits"; (e) as of 11 May 2026 AWS extended Activate credits to be redeemable for third-party models on Amazon Bedrock (previously credits could not cover third-party AI model usage). Student PROGRAM CHANGES: the YC AI Student Starter Pack was rebranded the "YC AI Stack" and is being 'rolled out' to more students in 2026; the live deals page's named credit amounts have rotated down from the Jan 2026 blog headline ($20K+ cloud / $5K+ GPT-Claude-Grok to currently ~$10K AWS + $1K OpenAI + $500 Anthropic + devtools), so the per-provider figures are moving. No genuine YC-branded free-tier or education-LICENSE program (in the software sense) exists — YC is a VC/accelerator, so all 'offers' are partner credit bundles delivered through YC.
Zoho Catalyst & Zoho ClassesNo 2025–2026 sunset of the core free offers; the changes were additive or model-level: (1) Zoho Catalyst Student Program ($250/6 months, no CC) is NEW — announced Sept 2, 2026, blogged Sept 6, 2026; nothing before it was retired. (2) Zoho for Startups: the old 'free Zoho One subscription for a year' was replaced by the wallet-credit model (FAQ explicitly calls Zoho One 'our old offering'); Stage 1/Stage 2 structure and 360-day validity are the current model. (3) Zoho Classes 2.0 relaunched July 15, 2026: pricing model flipped from per-student-per-year ($4/₹250, 2020 model) to per-instructor-per-month (Standard ~$7, Pro ~$20, press-reported) for private institutions; the 100-student free edition and the India government free-license offer were kept/strengthened — the government offer expanded explicitly to all Central & State government schools, colleges and universities at no licensing cost. (4) Catalyst historical price-change notice (effective Jan 1, 2024 / Apr 1, 2024 for pre-existing users) ended the free sandbox on Mar 31, 2024, introduced the monthly-renewing free tier and $5/project/month minimum billing on Apr 1, 2024 — still in force today, relevant gotcha for the free tier.
